# Trevor Robey, INC. — Shared Continuity Board

> **ONE living board, shared by all four Claude surfaces plus opencode.** This is the single
> source of truth for *what is being worked on right now* across the whole `TrevClaw-Obsidianodus`
> business stack. Every surface — **CLI Claude Code**, **Desktop Claude Code**, **Cowork**,
> **Claude Chat (Desktop)**, and **opencode** (via `~/.config/opencode/AGENTS.md`) — reads this
> file at the start of a session and appends to it when it makes meaningful progress or hands off.
>
> **Read this top-to-bottom before you touch anything.** How the system works, how each
> surface reaches this file, and the write-safety rules are in **[`README.md`](README.md)**.

- **Canonical path:** `/Users/myhome/TrevClaw-Obsidianodus/_continuity/CONTINUITY.md`
- **Repo-canonical, single file** — there is no mirror and no copy. All four surfaces edit *this* file on disk.
- **Business canon (do not duplicate here — point to it):** repo `CLAUDE.md`,
  `Trevor Robey Intelligence Network Citations/{THE-WHOLE-SYSTEM.md, SYSTEM-REALITY.md}`.

## 🧭 30-second protocol

1. **On start** → read **Current focus** + the top 3–5 **Log** entries. That is the live state.
2. **On meaningful progress or handoff** → **prepend** a Log entry (newest at top) using the
   template at the bottom. Keep it short and factual.
3. **Keep "Current focus" true to *now*** — update it (don't just append) so it always answers
   "what are we working on this minute?"
4. **Append, don't rewrite** other surfaces' Log entries. History is the point.
5. **Concurrency write caveat (updated 2026-07-26):** this file now lives on the **internal,
   FileVault-protected disk** — the old external-SSD hazards (disksleep `EPERM`, Chat-side writes
   wedging the Filesystem MCP) are gone. The remaining hazard is *four surfaces sharing one file*:
   **probe-first, prefer prepending one self-contained entry, verify-after** — full procedure in
   [`README.md`](README.md#write-safety-external-volume). If a surface is still reading this board
   from `/Volumes/ExtraCurricular`, it is on the **stale pre-migration copy** — repoint it.

> **Scope gate — business only:** write to this board **only when the work relates to the Trevor
> Robey, INC. business** — the citation engine, offerings/revenue/brand/legal, or this repo's own
> infra/topology (the `:8798` bridge, the vault, connectors, plugins). Unrelated work (home
> network, Security Everything, personal) belongs elsewhere, **not here.**

## 🧠 Two vaults — which one to write to (2026-07-26)

Every surface now sees **two** Perseus memory servers. They are not interchangeable:

| Write this | …to this server | Vault |
|---|---|---|
| Trevor Robey, INC. business facts | **`perseus-business`** | `~/.perseus-vault/data/business-vault.db` |
| Machine-wide facts (YubiKey gates, Falcon, browser lanes, host tooling) | `perseus-vault` | `~/.perseus-vault/data/perseus-vault.db` |

**Never write a business fact to the global vault.** Both are live here, so a stray write creates a
second copy that silently diverges the moment either is edited — the exact fragmentation this board
exists to prevent. The five business entities were migrated out of the global vault on 2026-07-26
and archived there; there is one source of truth per fact.

**This board is not replaced by either vault.** The board is the chronological narrative — Current
focus, workstreams, and a dated Log. A vault is a similarity-ranked fact store with no ordering and
active decay scoring, so it structurally cannot answer *"what are we working on right now."*
Board = the running story. Vault = the reference book. Keep writing both.

Reachability: `perseus-business` is registered in the repo's `.mcp.json` (Claude Code) **and** in
`claude_desktop_config.json` (Chat **and** Cowork — one file, same app). Its DB deliberately lives
outside the repo: `/Volumes/ExtraCurricular` has documented write hangs, and the FTS index stores
every memory in **plaintext**, which must never sit inside a git working tree.

### Three systems, three jobs — why they cannot overwrite each other

| System | Storage | Answers | Write style |
|---|---|---|---|
| **This board** | one markdown file, in git | *"what are we working on right now"* — ordered, dated, narrative | append/prepend; **overwrite only Current focus** |
| **`perseus-business`** | `business-vault.db` (+ own AES key) | *"what do we know about the business"* | idempotent per `category+key` |
| **`perseus-vault`** | `perseus-vault.db` (+ own AES key) | *"what do we know about this machine"* | idempotent per `category+key` |

They physically **cannot** overwrite one another — three separate stores, two of them separate
SQLite files with separate encryption keys, the third a git-tracked text file. The only real risks
are *logical*, and each has a guard:

- **Same fact in both vaults** → checked nightly by `~/.local/bin/perseus-vault-drift-check.py`,
  which fails **CRITICAL** on a `category+key` active in both, on a business topic sitting in the
  global vault (or vice-versa), and on the same summary copied under a renamed key. It **reports
  and refuses to fix** — choosing which vault owns a fact is a judgment call, and guessing deletes
  the copy someone wanted.
- **Two surfaces editing this board at once** → follow `README.md`'s safe-write pattern: probe with
  a read, prepend one self-contained entry rather than rewriting, verify after.
- **Board and vault duplicating content** → the board's standing rule: *point to canon, don't fork
  it.* Put the fact in a vault; put the pointer and the story here.

> **"Server disconnected" in the MCP logs is NOT a fault.** A Perseus stdio server self-exits after
> 600s idle — `no client activity for 600s — exiting idle stdio server (orphan-leak guard, #57228)`
> — and the app logs a red *Server disconnected*. By design; it is what keeps the process count
> bounded, and tools respawn on the next call. Do not "fix" it by setting
> `MIMIR_IDLE_TIMEOUT_SECS=0` — that reinstates the orphan leak.

## 🔒 Operating posture (inherited — enforce every task, all surfaces)

- **Research artifacts only** — not validated for clinical decision support.
- **No PHI** — public biomedical sources only.
- **Manual-only external actions** — no autonomous publish / post / login / transact.
- **Citation-traceability gate** — every citation resolves to a real PMID / NCT / ChEMBL ID /
  DOI. **Never fabricate a citation**; if none exists, say `[no verifiable citation found]`.
- Canon for the above: repo `CLAUDE.md` → "Rules" + "Security Orchestration";
  `Trevor Robey Intelligence Network Citations/SYSTEM-REALITY.md`.

### 2026-08-15T08:48Z · CLI Claude Code · **Raycast `summaryOutputFolder` was a phantom preference — declared in TS, absent from the manifest. Fixed, plus a guard test for the whole bug class**
- **SCOPE NOTE:** Copilot-Everything tooling (Raycast `obsidian-bridge-fork`); detail in project memory `raycast-obsidian-fork-bridge-video-pipeline`. Follow-on to the folder-rename entry below. **Current focus untouched**.
- **The defect:** `prefs.summaryOutputFolder` was read at runtime and declared on `VideoCommandPreferences`/`PdfCommandPreferences`, but was **never declared in `package.json`** — so Raycast never rendered a field, `getPreferenceValues()` always returned `undefined`, and the code silently took its hardcoded fallback every time. TypeScript cannot catch this: the hand-written interfaces type-check on their own and nothing cross-references the manifest Raycast actually reads.
- **Fixed:** `summaryOutputFolder` declared top-level (`textfield`, default `Copilot Summaries`). Auditing the interfaces found `summarizeTemplatePath` with the IDENTICAL defect — declared too, with **no default**, because `resolveSummaryTemplatePath` returns a configured value verbatim as an ABSOLUTE path, so a relative default would break it while blank preserves the existing vault-joined fallback exactly.
- **The durable part — `src/tests/preferenceManifest.spec.ts` (31 tests):** parses both preferences interfaces out of the source and asserts every field is declared at extension level or on the consuming command, with the failure message naming the exact consequence. It caught `summarizeTemplatePath` on its first run, which is why that one got fixed rather than exempted — narrowing the guard to dodge a known-bad field would have defeated its purpose.
- **Verified:** suite **393 passed / 6 skipped** (was 362) · `ray lint` clean incl. `validate package.json` and Prettier 2.8.8 · `ray build -e dev` deployed, installed manifest carries both prefs. **Proof it is genuinely wired: `raycast-env.d.ts` — Raycast's OWN codegen — went from having no such field at all to emitting `"summaryOutputFolder": string` NON-optional** (defaulted prefs are non-optional there; `"summarizeTemplatePath"?: string` stays optional). Absence from that generated file is the reliable tell for a phantom preference.
- **Residual, deliberate:** the summary destination and Copilot's `customCommandNoteFolder` are still two values that must agree — they do today (both `Copilot Summaries`), and the drift is now *visible and fixable in the Raycast prefs UI* rather than invisible in code. Making the pref fall through to Copilot's own setting when blank would collapse them to one source of truth; not done, as it changes resolution semantics. **Uncommitted.**

### 2026-08-11T02:45Z · opencode · **Qwen Fetch MCP fixed: mcp-server-fetch broken against mcp 2.x — wrapper package pins mcp<2 (verified handshake)**
- **SCOPE NOTE:** opencode↔Qwen MCP setup (the mcp-fetch-wrapper for the Fetch MCP server). **Current focus untouched** (founder lane).
- **Did:** the Fetch server showed `-32000 Connection closed`. Root cause: **mcp-server-fetch (all versions) declares `mcp>=1.1.3` with no upper bound**, but mcp 2.0.0 renamed `McpError` → `MCPError`; the fetch code still imports `McpError` → ImportError at startup → exit 1. Qwen's bundled uvx 0.6.16 also rejects multiple `--from` flags, so the pin can't be passed inline. Fix: built a tiny wrapper package at `/Users/myhome/My Installs/mcp-fetch-wrapper/` (`pyproject.toml` deps = `mcp-server-fetch>=2026.7.10` + `mcp>=1.1.3,<2`; `[project.scripts] qwen-mcp-fetch = fetch_entry:run` — a thin pass-through to `mcp_server_fetch.main()`). Verified end-to-end via Qwen's bundled uvx: `--from <wrapper> qwen-mcp-fetch` → initialize → `serverInfo: mcp-fetch v1.29.0`, tools/list → fetch tool. Fetch is NOT in settings.json mcp_config (only Goose is) — Qwen spawns it from its in-memory/leveldb registry, so the fix is applied via the UI.
- **UI recipe:** edit Fetch entry → Command dropdown = **uvx**; Parameters = three lines: `--from` / `/Users/myhome/My Installs/mcp-fetch-wrapper` / `qwen-mcp-fetch`; Environment variables = empty.
- **Touches:** `/Users/myhome/My Installs/mcp-fetch-wrapper/pyproject.toml` (new), `fetch_entry.py` (new).
- **For whoever's next:** the pattern is now: any stdio MCP server for Qwen must be a uvx-runnable local package (pyproject + console script). mcp 2.x is NOT backward compatible with servers importing `mcp.shared.exceptions.McpError` — pin `mcp<2` in the wrapper if the upstream package hasn't caught up.

### 2026-08-07T01:19Z · Desktop Claude Code · **Built `biomed-readonly` — a standalone read-only biomedical MCP server; free delegated worker now queries ChEMBL/CT.gov/PubMed/ICD-10 at $0**
- **SCOPE NOTE — machine-global Claude tooling (`~/.claude/mcp-servers/biomed-readonly-mcp/`); security artifacts in `~/Security Everything/opencode-delegation-guard/`** (no engine/business change; **Current focus untouched**). Fills the gap the 00:59Z entry named: structured biomedical DB research is now delegatable without touching the engine's coupled servers.
- **Built from scratch** (Python + official `mcp` SDK pinned `<2` + stdlib `urllib`, own venv): `server.py` (7 FastMCP tools) + `sources.py` (per-API fns + egress allowlist) + README + requirements. Tools: `chembl_search_molecule/target`, `ctgov_search/get`, `pubmed_search`, `icd10_search/lookup`. All GET-only against public keyless APIs.
- **Security (passes the Classification Rule):** no POST/PUT/DELETE path anywhere; **egress host allowlist** (only ebi.ac.uk / clinicaltrials.gov / eutils.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov / clinicaltables.nlm.nih.gov) with an https-only + host assertion before every request; **no tool accepts a raw URL** → SSRF/exfil structurally impossible (verified: `127.0.0.1` refused); no creds/secrets; clamps + truncation + 15s timeout; fail-soft `{ok:false}` never crashes stdio. Deliberately **NOT** double-registered in Claude host or the engine — lives only in `~/.claude/mcp-servers/` + the opencode guardrail.
- **ICD-10 = NLM Clinical Tables, not WHO** (WHO needs OAuth → fails the public-data rule). Its titles are anatomical, so a histology term like *glioblastoma* returns 0 by design — search "malignant neoplasm brain" or `icd10_lookup("C71.9")`.
- **Verified:** stdio initialize+tools/list (7 tools); live calls to all four sources (NCT01609790, EGFR targets, real PubMed DOIs, ICD-10 diabetes=481/C71.9); egress guard refuses loopback. Registered in the guardrail (`enabled:true`) → `opencode mcp list` shows biomed-readonly + searxng + scholar **connected**, 11 dangerous **disabled**. **End-to-end through the real `server.py`: a free `delegate` worker called `biomed-readonly_ctgov_search`, returned NCT01609790 + title, no dangerous tool, cost $0.**
- **State:** done & verified. Integrity baseline at `~/Security Everything/opencode-delegation-guard/BIOMED-MANIFEST.sha256`. No monorepo/git changes (all under `~/.claude` + `~/.config` + `~/Security Everything`).
- **For whoever's next:** to add a source, keep it GET-only + add its host to `ALLOWED_HOSTS` in `sources.py`; never add a tool that takes a caller-supplied URL. Optional `NCBI_API_KEY`/`NCBI_EMAIL` env raises PubMed limits (default absent).

### 2026-07-31T21:40Z · CLI Claude Code · **Obsidian MCP topology audited (read-only) — "Local REST API with MCP" is ALREADY the vault engine, and the Copilot fork's own bridge calls are broken**
- **SCOPE NOTE — `Copilot-Everything` workspace infra** (`:8798` bridge + vault plugins), in board scope per the topology clause. No business-facing change; **Current focus untouched**. Detail in project memory `local-rest-api-is-the-vault-engine`.
- **Question asked:** could the community *Local REST API with MCP* plugin replace the vendored Copilot MCP fork? **Answer: it is not an alternative — it is already the layer underneath.** `obsidian_health` live: every one of the six vault operations routes `restApi_selected_via_priority` to **`https://127.0.0.1:27124`**, LRA **v5.0.3**. The fork is an MCP *client*; LRA is the MCP *server*. Only the ~200-line `RestApiBackend` shim could be retired, and doing so would cost the HMAC replay guard, `miyo_search_context`, the dev-console tools and the single canonical `:8798` access point.
- **🔴 LIVE DEFECT FOUND — the fork's in-Obsidian vault tools fail on every call.** The deployed plugin sends `backendHint:"cli"` (baked into `main.js`, set in `data.json` for both servers), but the bridge's dispatcher accepts only the literal `"restApi"` — the CLI backend was removed **2026-05-09**. A/B proved it live: with the hint → **HTTP 400** `invalid_literal`; identical body without it → **HTTP 200** via `restApi`. Claude surfaces are unaffected (the stdio adapter sends no hint), and `:8799` ignores the field, so only Copilot-chat's seven `obsidian_*` tools are dead. Spawned as a separate task; **nothing changed here**.
- **Two stale docs confirmed:** `Copilot-Everything/CLAUDE.md` still says *"backend priority is `restApi → cli`"* — `backendSelector.ts` has been **REST-only** since 2026-05-09 and its constructor *throws* without `restApi`. And the deployed `copilot/main.js` (built Jul 16) still carries a hardcoded `vaultRoot:"/Volumes/ExtraCurricular/My Digital Garden"` default — inert because `data.json` overrides it, but it would resurrect the pre-move path on any settings reset.

### 2026-07-31T03:20Z · CLI Claude Code · **Graveyard-memo census shipped — and it turned up a LIVE overclaim in the AWS Data Exchange listing**
- **Backlog item #5 done.** The stopped-trial register is now an exhaustive census for the condition query; protocol-churn stays a bounded sample. Verified live: **glioblastoma 390 of 390, `censusComplete=true`, ~7 s** (was 8 records).
- **🔴 This was a CORRECTNESS fix, not an enhancement.** Measured on one ordered glioblastoma cohort, the shipped `buildAmendmentRisk()` returned **MODERATE at n=5, MODERATE at n=8, LOW at n=10, MODERATE at n=20, HIGH at n=40.** The amendment band in a **$14,950** deliverable was a function of sample size, not of the indication. It is still climbing at n=40, so the memo now states its sample size and the band's sensitivity rather than implying the number is settled.
- **🔴 THE BIGGER FIND — the AWS Data Exchange listing was selling a completeness it could not deliver.** `README.md` said *"**ALL** stopped trials for the TA"*, `offers.md` said *"(complete TA)"* as an **offer term**, and the generated `SAMPLE-NOTICE.md` said *"the complete feed (all N …)"* where **N was the clamped number**. Cause: a **DOUBLE clamp** — `build-graveyard-feed.js` defaulted `--pageSize` to 20 with a hard cap of 50, on top of the fetcher's own 50. Fingerprint visible in the committed data: **every one of the six shipped TAs is exactly 50 rows.** README also claimed glioblastoma had *"12 stopped trials"* — a third number matching neither the 50-row file nor the 390 population. Copy corrected in all four surfaces; **the `data/` tree still predates the fix and MUST be regenerated before any paid offer publishes** — flagged in a warning box in README and inline in offers.md. Nothing was published (price still `[DECIDE]`), so this was caught pre-sale.
- **Six blocking defects from three adversarial lenses, all fixed before writing:** (1) the pagination loop could **never terminate** — duplicate-dropping means a page can add zero records while still advancing the token, so a `records.length` guard can't fire; added a page ceiling **and** a no-progress break. (2) An earlier draft **silently deleted** the most-amended-modules sentence, leaving `topModules` computed and unused — a content regression inside an honesty fix. (3) *"the whole registered population"* was wrong: `query.cond` is **term-matched**, and *"NSCLC"* returns **1,401** where *"non-small cell lung cancer"* returns **1,391** — every completeness claim is now bound to the **query**, with that caveat rendering unconditionally. (4) `censusComplete` could be true while N < M, producing self-contradicting output; it now requires the count to actually reach `countTotal`, with a third "walked-but-short" branch that refuses to name a cause it cannot know. (5) The §4(a) binding copy rule asserted completeness unconditionally — now a **two-branch rule keyed on `censusComplete`**, lifted verbatim from the artifact, never hand-composed. (6) **The plan would have RAISED the prompt cap from 50 to 1000 while claiming to add a guard** — forcing `census:false` constrains the flag, not `pageSize`. Now two separate ceilings (`PROMPT_MAX_PAGE_SIZE` 50 vs `CTGOV_MAX_PAGE_SIZE` 1000) enforced in the fetcher **and** re-clamped at the local-fetcher seam. Attack tested: `pageSize:1000` and `pageSize:99999` both return 50.
- **Plus one bug of my own, caught by a fixture:** `Number(null)` is `0` and `Number.isFinite(0)` is true, so a missing denominator rendered *"1 of null record(s)"*. Fixed and pinned.
- **Also fixed a pre-existing silent defect:** the memo rode the scout prefetch array, so on 6 of 7 templates it was built from scout's **six** records while the copy rules said eight. The size-aware cache key ends that coupling.
- **Verified:** `npm test` **1036/1036, 0 fail** (was 1023 — +13 regression tests, one per defect) · `phase2:gates` **exit 0** · restricted-globals + transport linters clean · `audit:connectors` clean, 12 prefetch tools referenced · live census + the full generator re-run end-to-end.
- **Touched:** `ctgov-supplemental.js` (rewritten), `graveyard-memo.js` (rewritten), `workflow-engine.js`, `_workflow-runtime.js`, `build-graveyard-feed.js`, both test files, `connector-registry.json`, founder-lane §4(a)+§6, root `CLAUDE.md`, and the three AWS listing files. **Nothing committed.**
- **✅ FOLLOW-ON, SAME SESSION — all six TAs regenerated, so the artifact now matches the corrected copy.** Original condition strings recovered from each `summary.json` (the slugs are lossy — re-running with a different query string would silently produce a different population). Every one `censusComplete=true`: **pancreatic cancer 734 · Alzheimer disease 428 · glioblastoma 390 · sickle cell disease 179 · ALS 114 · IPF 76 — 1,921 paid rows, up from 300.** Verified: `citation-index.csv` scales 1:1 (390/390, 734/734); free-vs-paid headers **identical** (the listing's "identical schema" claim holds); 390/390 NCT-shaped ids, 0 malformed. The README warning box is now a RESOLVED box with the before/after table, and `offers.md`'s "do not publish until regenerated" block is retired. **The paid offer is no longer blocked by a false completeness claim** — remaining blockers are the price (`[DECIDE]`) and AWS's own product review.
- **Gotcha worth keeping:** a Bash command containing a `fetch` token **and** a later `|` is process-blocked by Falcon rule 1 — empty output, exit 1, reads exactly like a crash. It cost one debug cycle here; write the script to a file with the Write tool instead of heredoc-plus-pipe.
- **For whoever's next:** **a cap in code becomes a claim in copy.** The 50-row clamp did not just under-deliver — it propagated into a marketplace offer term, a product description and a generated buyer notice, each of which then read as deliberate. When you lift a cap, grep the sales surfaces in the same pass.

### 2026-07-30T23:50Z · CLI Claude Code · **`trial-design-benchmark` template BUILT — the $4,950 SKU is engine-producible for the first time; the cohort's selection rule is now part of the product**
- **Built** the top item in the founder-lane backlog. Trial Design Benchmark Pack had **no workflow template, no mission template, no intake path**; it now has all three, plus a CT.gov design-cohort fetcher and a deterministic no-LLM renderer. This is the SKU that fits inside a single **$6,500 SBIR Phase I TABA** vendor line, so it is what opens the Phase I founder tier.
- **Method:** 10-agent workflow (6 parallel contract readers → plan → 3 adversarial lenses). **5 blocking defects**, all verified against live CT.gov calls, all fixed before anything was written.
- **🔴 The sharpest defect — the cohort was an arbitrary page under an inflated denominator.** The first design sent **no status filter and no sort**. Live: `query.cond='non-small cell lung cancer'&aggFilters=phase:3&countTotal=true` → **totalCount 1014**; adding `filter.overallStatus=COMPLETED,ACTIVE_NOT_RECRUITING,RECRUITING,ENROLLING_BY_INVITATION` → **668**. So ~**34%** of an unfiltered "eligible comparable programs" figure is withdrawn/terminated/suspended/unknown or never started, and an unsorted page returned a **1995 trial with status UNKNOWN** and a **WITHDRAWN trial with enrollment 0**. Fixed: explicit filter **and** `sort=StartDate:desc`, both returned to the renderer and **printed** — *"20 of 668"* is meaningless without saying which 20.
- **🔴 Survivorship trap.** A WITHDRAWN trial reports `enrollmentInfo.count = 0` — a *present* number — so a naive "median over trials reporting a count" folds trials that never enrolled a patient into the median (fixture proved it: **400 instead of 800**). Summary stats now run over a `statsCohort` excluding WITHDRAWN/NOT_YET_RECRUITING/SUSPENDED/TERMINATED/UNKNOWN; those rows stay in the **table** with status shown, and the exclusion is printed.
- **🟠 Honest cohort size.** Fetcher default is **20, deliberately NOT the 8** `ctgov_stopped_trials` uses — copying that default would silently under-deliver a pack promising 10–20 comparators. Thin indications can't supply 10–20 at any page size (**FOP = 4 eligible, Creutzfeldt-Jakob = 1**), so the renderer emits a cohort-size notice saying it covers the **entire eligible population**. **All four** canon copies of "10–20 comparable programs" corrected to *"up to 20 … N of M eligible"* (`OFFERINGS.md`, `REVENUE_PLAYBOOK.md`, `product-catalog.md`, `CLAUDE.md`).
- **🟠 `CLAUDE.md`'s "PDF + Tableau workbook" delivery claim was UNBUILDABLE** — `export-release.js` emits only `folder`/`zip`, the sole npm dependency is `zod`, and "tableau" appears nowhere in the codebase. Corrected to `design-benchmark.md` + `design-benchmark.csv` in a manual ZIP.
- **🟠 Two traps worth remembering.** (1) `intake.html`'s `val()` is a **document-wide first-match** `querySelector`; reusing `name="indication"`/`"drug_class"` would have been shadowed by earlier panels, making the required-field check *permanently* fail and the mailto carry blanks — fields are `tdb_*`. (2) The phase33 test iterates a **hardcoded** template array, not `readdirSync`, so a new template is invisible to the suite until appended.
- **Citation scope:** the benchmark is a **post-gate deterministic render** — `citation-gate.js` reads only subtask `output_path` files, so a packager-written file can never be inside the grade. Grounded by construction, **not graded**, ships with a grade-adjacency disclosure. The intake page deliberately shows **no** conditional panel for this SKU, because `panel-h2` advertises the Citation-Integrity grade and pairing it with this SKU would be that same overclaim in sales form.
- **Verified:** template validates against the Zod schema · **phase30 30/30** · **phase33 28/28** (was 27) · **`npm test` 1023/1023, 0 fail** (was 1022) · **`phase2:gates` exit 0** · restricted-globals + transport linters clean · `audit:connectors` all 12 prefetch tools registered · live render produced real deliverables for both a dense (20/668) and a thin (4/4, shortfall notice fired) indication. **Nothing committed.**
- **✅ END-TO-END RUN PASSED (operator armed the gate 2026-07-30).** `run-workflow.js --template trial-design-benchmark --inputs '{"indication":"non-small cell lung cancer"}' --operator-approve --confirm` → **exit 0, all six steps completed**, offer `offer-3dec35098e8e47e2d0a72012`, 126 KB export zip. **Citation gate PASSED: integrity grade A — 50 citations, 50 grounded (100%), 0 unverified, 0 retracted**, all three subtasks "Comprehensive" coverage. **`design-benchmark.md` (9 KB) + `design-benchmark.csv` (6 KB) shipped in `deliverables/`**, both auto-sealed into `manifest.json` and `checksums.txt` by `finalizeBundle`'s directory walk (no explicit registration needed, as designed). Shipped content verified: *"Cohort: 20 of 668 eligible"*, the selection rule + `sort=StartDate:desc` printed, the citation-scope disclosure present — sitting directly beneath an **A grade**, which is exactly the adjacency it exists to disambiguate. Table carries 20 real NCT-linked programs (Merck, BMS, Boehringer, Astellas, NCI); summary reports 20/20 randomized, median enrollment 470 (range 230–1410), PARALLEL 19/20. **Seed doc carried NO unresolved `{{...}}`** — the `description_template` fix held. **✅ INTAKE FORM ALSO VERIFIED** (operator armed `cb` 2026-07-30): radio checks · only the `trial-design-benchmark` field-group becomes visible · `val("tdb_indication")` returns the typed text while `val("indication")` returns `""` (proving the shadowing defect is gone) · submit validator passes · mailto carries both fields · panel switching is clean · **and the negative case fails correctly** when the required field is emptied, which a shadowed lookup could not do. Screenshot confirms *"Selected: Trial Design Benchmark Pack — $4,950"*. **Nothing about this build is now unverified.**
- **For whoever's next:** **a deliverable's selection rule is part of the deliverable.** "Top N" with no stated filter or sort isn't a benchmark, it's a sample of unknown provenance — and printing an unfiltered `countTotal` as the eligible pool inflates it by a third.

Checked the other four ids (`obsidian-bridge-launcher`, `vault-zettelkasten`, `contextual-wiki-definitions`, `origin-nest`): **no collisions.** Rationale recorded in `Claude/obsidian-command-center/CLAUDE.md`.

Blast radius was small only because the author made it fail-soft on purpose (`src/main.ts:357`); notes, Miyo, Zettelkasten and memory-ingest were unaffected, and no run had actually degraded yet (last run 07-24, pre-move). **Redeployed; `ExtraCurricular` count 2 → 0.** Root cause worth remembering: **`obsidian-command-center` has no `deploy` script** (only dev/build/lint:transport), so its copy is manual — unlike `obsidian-bridge-launcher`, which has `npm run deploy`.

The pattern: almost every "vulnerability" collapsed to *"attacker must already have UID-501 code execution"*, at which point they read the vault directly. Positively verified: all listeners loopback-bound; `:8798` `pathGuard.ts:26` rejects dot-segments incl. `.obsidian`; `:8799` wildcard CORS sets **no** `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials`, structurally closing the usual escalation; mkcert SAN pinned to `localhost`/`127.0.0.1` defeats DNS rebinding; `enableInsecureServer:false` so plaintext `:27123` never listens. **Applied anyway as hygiene:** `execSync` → `execFileSync` in `obsidian-bridge-launcher/src/main.ts` (both `lsof` call sites — no `/bin/sh`, so a tampered `bridgePort` can't be shell-interpreted) plus a `bridgePort` coercion in `loadSettings()` that **emits a `settings_coerced` audit event** instead of failing silently; and the **vault root `0707` → `0700`** (it was masked only by `/Users/myhome` being `0750` — a bad thing to rely on).

### 2026-07-26T08:15Z · CLI Claude Code · **The "perseus-business disconnected" log is BY DESIGN** — plus a cross-vault drift detector so the three memory systems can never silently collide
- **The disconnect is not a fault.** The operator flagged `[perseus-business] Server disconnected` in the Desktop MCP log. The server printed its own reason one line earlier: `mimir: no client activity for 600s — exiting idle stdio server (orphan-leak guard, #57228)`. Timestamps confirm it exactly — last tool call `07:59:23.041`, transport closed `08:09:23.052` = **600.011s**. This is the documented behaviour in the global `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`: stdio servers self-reap when idle, the app logs a red "Server disconnected", and tools respawn on the next call. It is what keeps the process count bounded. **Do not "fix" it with `MIMIR_IDLE_TIMEOUT_SECS=0`** — that reinstates the orphan leak. Verified healthy before the reap: `Initializing → encryption enabled → local ONNX embedding enabled → MCP server ready → tools/list ok`.
- **Verified `perseus-business` really did load on every surface** — not from config, from the process table. Claude.app had **5 business servers and 5 global servers running, perfectly paired**: two pairs parented to `Claude` (Chat + Cowork), two via the `disclaimer` helper, one to `claude` (CLI). This also closes the open question from the previous entry: no `~/.claude.json` user-scope entry is needed, so repo-scoping on Claude Code survives.
- **New: `~/.local/bin/perseus-vault-drift-check.py`.** The routing rule (business → `perseus-business`, machine-wide → `perseus-vault`) lived only in prose, and prose is advisory — both vaults are live in this repo simultaneously, so nothing stopped a stray write from creating a second copy that diverges on the next edit. The detector now runs nightly inside `perseus-vault-maintain.py` and fails **CRITICAL** on three shapes: (1) **COLLISION** — same `category+key` active in both vaults; (2) **MISROUTE** — a `trevclaw/*`/`business/*` topic active in the global vault, or a `security/*`/`operator/*`/`claude-code/*`/`tooling/*` topic in the business vault; (3) **ECHO** — the same summary copied across under a *renamed* key, which key comparison alone would miss.
- **Detect-only, deliberately.** The embedding backfill beside it auto-repairs because a poisoned vector has exactly one correct fix. Drift has none: deciding which vault *owns* a fact is a judgment call, and guessing deletes the copy someone wanted. So it reports and stops.
- **State:** all three checks proven on **copies**, never the live vaults — un-archiving a migrated entity triggered COLLISION+MISROUTE (exit 2); writing a renamed duplicate through a keyed server triggered ECHO with `COLLISION: 0`, proving the third check earns its place. Live vaults are **clean**: global 16 active, business 6 active, 0 collisions, 0 misroutes, 0 echoes, embeddings 21/0 and 6/0 poisoned. Nightly job exit 0 across both vaults + drift.
- **Also:** the "Two vaults" section above gained a **three-systems separation table** — board vs `perseus-business` vs `perseus-vault`, what each answers, and why they physically cannot overwrite each other (three separate stores; two SQLite files with *separate* AES keys; one git-tracked text file). The only real risks are logical, and each now names its guard.
- **Touches:** new `~/.local/bin/perseus-vault-drift-check.py`; `~/.local/bin/perseus-vault-maintain.py` (drift step + CRITICAL log); this board (separation table + the disconnect note + this entry).
- **For whoever's next:** if the nightly job ever logs `vault_drift`, it is telling you two stores now disagree about the same fact — pick the owner, `forget` it from the other, and re-run `perseus-vault-drift-check.py`. Gotcha met twice while testing: **SQLite refuses direct writes to FTS5 shadow tables** (`entities_fts_content may not be modified`), so you cannot seed FTS-based test fixtures by hand — write through a *keyed server* instead, which is also the only path that decrypts.

### 2026-07-26T07:20Z · CLI Claude Code · **Business memory split into its own vault, then seated on all three surfaces** — plus a silent-corruption bug found and fixed in Perseus embeddings
- **Did:** (1) Enabled Perseus **semantic search** — local in-process ONNX (`all-MiniLM-L6-v2`, 384-dim), no network, no Ollama. Two blockers, not one: the missing `--embedding-model` flag, and — hidden behind it — the binary's embedded Python shim feeds only `input_ids`+`attention_mask` while the stock HF ONNX **requires `token_type_ids`**. Patched the graph so that input is synthesized internally as zeros (correct, not approximate, for single-sentence embedding; verified **bit-identical**, `max|delta| = 0.000e+00`). (2) Created **`perseus-business`** — its own DB (`~/.perseus-vault/data/business-vault.db`) and its own AES key — and migrated the 5 business entities out of the global vault, archiving the originals there so there is **one source of truth**. (3) Registered it in the repo `.mcp.json` **and** `claude_desktop_config.json`, which the SearXNG 2026-07-04 entry below confirms is shared by **Chat *and* Cowork** (same app: that file carries `coworkScheduledTasksEnabled`/`coworkWebSearchEnabled`). (4) Taught the nightly launchd job to maintain **both** vaults and to **auto-repair** embeddings, logging what it changed.
- **⚠️ The find that matters: `perseus_vault_embed` POISONS an encrypted vault.** It reads `body_json` raw and embeds it **without decrypting**, so stored vectors describe base64 ciphertext. Measured: `cos(stored, embed(ciphertext)) = +1.000000` vs `+0.18` for the real text. `semantic_search` then returns a full, confident, well-formed result set ranked by **noise** — no error, no empty result. `perseus_vault_remember` is safe (embeds plaintext before encrypting, `cos +1.000000`). **Never call `perseus_vault_embed` on either vault.** Repair/verify with `~/.local/bin/perseus-vault-embed-backfill.py` (`--check` = tripwire, exit 2 if poisoned).
- **State:** done + verified. Both vaults **0 poisoned** (global 16 active/5 archived, business 5). Business server spawns correctly from *both* registrations; a no-keyword-overlap query ("must every local service use encrypted transport here") returns `https-only-invariant` first. Nightly job exit 0 across both vaults, incl. via `launchctl kickstart`. Poison drill: seeded corruption was detected, auto-repaired, and logged by entity name. Fail-closed proven per-vault (business key removed → global still ran, exit 78).
- **Also fixed:** the pre-existing FTS tripwire opened `mode=ro`, which **cannot open a WAL DB when `-shm` is absent** — so on genuinely corrupted data it logged "failed to read database" instead of CRITICAL, hiding the exact thing it existed to catch. Now falls back to a SELECT-only connection and counts **all** rows, not one.
- **Next (operator):** restart Claude Desktop so Chat + Cowork load `perseus-business`. Then **verify whether the Desktop Code tab sees it** — it lives in repo `.mcp.json`, and the SearXNG precedent registered `~/.claude.json` separately "for CLI + Desktop Code tab", so the Code tab may need a user-scope entry (cost: it becomes global for Code too). **Not added pre-emptively — verify first.**
- **Touches:** `~/Library/Application Support/Claude/claude_desktop_config.json` (+`perseus-business`, backed up), repo `.mcp.json`, repo `CLAUDE.md` (new "🧠 Business memory" section), this board (new "🧠 Two vaults" section above), `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`, `~/.local/bin/perseus-vault-{maintain,embed-backfill}.py`, `~/.perseus-vault/{models/,embed-venv/,business.key}`.
- **For whoever's next:** the routing rule is **business facts → `perseus-business`, machine-wide → `perseus-vault`** (see the new section near the top of this board). The business DB deliberately sits **outside** the repo — `/Volumes/ExtraCurricular` has documented write hangs, and the FTS5 index keeps every memory in **plaintext**, which must never enter a git tree. Two more traps: `perseus-vault keygen` **defaults `--key-file` to the existing `secret.key`** (running it bare destroys the main vault's key — always pass an explicit path); and `vault-export`/`obsidian-sync` accept only `--db`, **no `--encryption-key`**, so exporting an encrypted vault via the CLI silently writes ciphertext. This board is **not** replaced by the vault — recall is similarity-ranked with decay, so it cannot answer "what are we working on right now."

- **Wiki-definition plugin v1.6.0 (signed `0f8ed91`)** — VZ enrichment was *degrading* notes. Three root causes fixed: (1) **wrong subject** — the call passed only `{notePath: origin}`, so VZ analysed the ORIGIN note (observed: a section headed `Echo Chamber Analysis for "Define General Intelligence"` inside an *Artificial Intelligence* note); now passes `noteContent` = the fresh definition (returned verbatim, no file access → also sidesteps the un-indexed-new-note problem) while `notePath` stays on the indexed origin for Miyo grounding. (2) **structural corruption** — VZ emitted `## ` headings into a section body whose matcher is `\n## `-bounded, which **emptied three of five sections** and ballooned a note 12,232 → 28,688 bytes / 24 H2s; `_sanitizeTraitOutput()` now demotes headings to bold (stripping inner emphasis), strips scaffolding, and caps at 1,800 chars. (3) **no quality bar** — `_traitGate()` requires ≥2 wikilinks, zero headings, the term named early/repeatedly, ≥60% of what it would overwrite, and rejects text opening with the ORIGIN note's name. Verified against the **real captured damage**: it sanitizes 3,774 → 1,768 chars (6 headings → 0) and is **REJECTED**, while a genuine grounded upgrade still passes.
- **Root cause fixed upstream too:** all six VZ trait prompts said *"Structure your response:"* + a numbered bold-header list, and had **zero** output-format constraints — so the model was primed to emit headings. Replaced with bold-labelled-bullets phrasing + a new `outputContract()` (no headings, bullets only, no preamble, `[[wikilinks]]`, <250 words) on all six.
- **ARCHITECTURE.md rewritten (signed `cbad192`, 429 → 289 lines)** from a 5-agent read-only survey of live code + running processes. The old file was a 2026-04-12 "Phase-35 snapshot" describing scout→Ollama, Traefik failover, Prometheus/Grafana, PM2, TrevNodus and pre-flat-merge folder names. Now documents the real invocation contract, the six templates' single six-step dependency graph, verbatim routing (`phase34-role-provider-map-v1`; every fallback `""` on purpose), how `claude -p` is actually spawned (stdin-piped, flat Max billing), the seven blocking `PHASE30_` codes + fail-closed **0.75** grounded floor + three-state verdict, which deliverables are deterministic/no-LLM, and a **"Retired — do not reintroduce"** table.
- **Verification:** engine suite **939/939 pass**; transport gate **exit 0**; both MCP bridges return `ok:true` on real tool calls (`:8798` vault search hit; `:8799` 11 tools enumerated). Commits: `0f8ed91` (plugin repo), `a55b002` (Ollama removal), `cbad192` (architecture) — all GPG-signed.

### 2026-07-16 · CLI Claude Code · Fixed a citation-metric MISLABEL: surface the real integrity gate, stop calling density "confidence"
- **Caught during the first live run's review (heart-failure clinical-trial-landscape).** The pipeline pane + notes showed "Confidence: 1.00 | 29/94 | PASS" — but per the engine's OWN code (`workflow-engine.js:723-730`) that number is a citation-**DENSITY heuristic** (citation-shaped lines ÷ total lines, `×10` then capped at 1.0, so **any report with ≥10% density shows "1.00"**), NOT verification. The engine says surface it "as citation density, **never as confidence**"; the Command Center note-gen mislabeled it — reviving the disavowed "1.0 Quality Confidence Score" overclaim — and never surfaced the REAL gate.
- **The real gate is separate and this run ACED it:** `artifacts.citation_verification` (`core/citation-gate.js`) = **verdict PASS, 47/47 cited IDs grounded (100%), 0 ungrounded, 0 retracted, floor 0.75.** That's the truth gate backing zero-fabrication.

### 2026-07-12 · CLI Claude Code · First business connector keyed securely — Scholar Sidekick bearer key wired zero-plaintext (reusable secret pattern)
- **Did:** Wired the operator's Scholar Sidekick `ssk_` API key onto the existing `scholar-sidekick` remote http MCP (`~/.claude.json`, was anonymous) — raising the 500-req/mo cap and enabling the PMID-retraction check that closes a real Phase-30 gate hole. **The key never touched the assistant** (hard rule): operator stored it via hidden Keychain prompt; assistant only wired a `${VAR}` reference.
- **Reusable secure pattern (use for every future keyed connector):** (1) `security add-generic-password -U -a "$USER" -s <svc> -w` (silent prompt — no echo, no history, no assistant exposure); (2) publish to the GUI-session env from Keychain via a login LaunchAgent (`launchctl setenv <VAR> "$(security find-generic-password …-w)"`, `RunAtLoad`) — value at rest ONLY in Keychain; (3) header `Authorization: Bearer ${VAR}` via `claude mcp add … --header '…${VAR}…'` **single-quoted** so it stores literally. Verified: `grep -c 'ssk_' ~/.claude.json` = **0**, reference present = 1.
- **Verified:** Claude Code DOES expand `${VAR}` in http headers from **process.env** (stores the header literally; canary-tested). Live `resolveIdentifier` through the Desktop MCP host returned full CSL metadata — connector functional.
- **🔑 Gotcha (reusable):** `${VAR}` resolves from the **connecting process's own env**, not from `launchctl getenv`. So the **Desktop app must be FULLY quit + reopened AFTER `launchctl setenv`** to inherit it (a plain window-close/reload won't). The `claude` CLI shows `[Warning] Missing environment variables` unless its own shell has the var (add the Keychain `export` to `~/.zshrc` if you use the CLI). Authed-vs-anonymous is not observable from the tool result — confirm on scholar-sidekick.com/account usage.
- **State:** config wiring done; commit not applicable (`~/.claude.json` is not a repo file). Next connectors follow the same pattern; see memory `secure-mcp-bearer-key-pattern`.

### 2026-07-10 · Desktop Claude Code · Q8 resolved — 0 is doctrine-required; the real bug was a mislabel (Q1–Q9 all done)
- **The "obvious fix" would have been a doctrine violation.** Q8 asked whether `max_unchecked_ratio=0` is too strict. Tracing the code: `delivery_decision` gates nothing except BLOCK (`workflow-engine.js:446`) — DELIVER and DELIVER_WITH_FLAGS ship identically; the threshold only sets the manifest's claim-strength *label*. Since `DELIVER` = the "every id independently re-resolved" claim, a tolerance > 0 would let that claim appear when untrue (an overclaim). So **0 is doctrine-REQUIRED**, kept + documented.
- **The genuine bug:** `unchecked_over_threshold` (a soft flag on a report that SHIPS) was pushed into `block_reasons` and rendered in the customer-facing manifest as if it blocked. Fixed: split `flag_reasons` (soft, ships) from `block_reasons` (hard stop); a shipping report now carries no block reason, and the markdown adds a "This report SHIPS … NOT a defect … still fully GROUNDED" clarification for DELIVER_WITH_FLAGS. `block_reasons` has no consumer outside the manifest → contained.
- **Verified:** 38 tests + lint + phase30 gate green. **Q1–Q9 now all resolved.** Remaining code-actionable: Q11/Q12 (quality_audit "confidence" naming), Q37 (full-text scan on manual path). The rest (Q10, Q13–Q36, Q38) are operator/business/legal decisions. Follow-up: re-resolver retry to reduce transient UNCHECKED (earns clean DELIVERs honestly, never by lowering the bar).

### 2026-07-10 · CLI Claude Code · PMID-regex bounds fixed — sub-6-digit + future-digit PMIDs now visible; **zero grounded-ratio impact** (quantified)
- **Did:** `citation-extractor.js` PMID pattern `\d{6,9}` → **`\d{1,10}(?!\d)`**. Fixes both bugs the task named: (a) sub-6-digit historical PMIDs were invisible to BOTH the grounding gate and the retraction screen (**PMID 1 confirmed live = a real 1975 paper**), so a retracted 1970s paper cited by a Failed Trial Archaeology report slipped through; (b) `\d{6,9}` TRUNCATED a 10+ digit run to a 9-digit prefix that can collide with a *different* real PMID — the `(?!\d)` lookahead now DROPS an over-long run instead of mis-capturing a prefix.
- **Decision (task asked me to choose the lower bound):** lower bound = **1**. The mandatory `PMID:` prefix makes stray-number false positives rare, and any false positive is **self-correcting** (an unfetched id is flagged ungrounded, never silently trusted) — whereas a *missed* retracted historical paper is not. Upper bound 10 (centuries of headroom past the current ~41M/8-digit max) with anti-truncation lookahead.
- **Impact quantified on REAL data (the task's key ask):** ran old vs new regex over all 57 shipped-bundle files (33 PMID matches) → **delta 0, nothing added, nothing dropped**. Real reports cite 7–8 digit PMIDs both patterns already matched. So the grounded-ratio denominator is unchanged for every shipping report; this is a pure correctness fix for edge cases, no gate weakening.
- **Verified:** 101/101 tests across extractor + gate + retraction + manifest; new extractor regressions (short PMID, 9/10-digit future, 11-digit-drops-not-truncates, stops-at-non-digit); end-to-end live (real NCBI) — PMID 1 now extracted → screened → not retracted → definitively checked. Perf: linear, no lookahead backtracking (100k-digit run <1ms). Change is the PMID line ONLY — no other identifier pattern touched.
- **Touches:** `openclaw-bridge/core/citation-extractor.js`, `tests/core/citation-extractor.test.js`, `SYSTEM-REALITY.md`. Uncommitted (commit needs YubiKey signature). Both spawned follow-up tasks from the retraction work are now DONE (DOI-only Wakefield gap + this PMID-regex task).

### 2026-07-10 · CLI Claude Code · Retraction-gate hardening — adversarial review of the Wakefield fix caught 5 issues, all fixed + a ⚠️ cross-surface heads-up
- **Ran a 4-lens adversarial review** of the DOI→PMID escalation / notice-disambiguation / paren-DOI extractor code (same pattern that caught a critical bug last round). It confirmed **5 findings; all now fixed + tested:**
  1. **(major) fail-soft guard** — `screenDoisViaPubmed` trusted a DOI→PMID row's `pmids` even when `resolved:false` (a transport failure), which a hostile/buggy resolver could turn into a manufactured retraction. Now PMIDs are trusted ONLY when `resolved===true`. (Latent — the shipped resolver never emits that shape — but a real contract gap.)
  2. **(major) NCBI rate-limit** — the escalation fired up to 50 sequential unthrottled esearch calls, blowing past NCBI's 3/sec and self-degrading the screen to `INCOMPLETE` every run. Added a per-instance throttle to `pubmed-retraction.js` (default OFF so offline tests stay instant; production wiring sets **350ms anon / 110ms with `NCBI_API_KEY`**). Verified with an injected fake clock (no real delays in tests).
  3. **(minor) O(n²) → O(n)** — `normalizeDoiId` rescanned the whole string per trailing `)`; adversarial input (64k stray `)`) took **49s → now 4.3ms**.
  4. **(minor) uncapped escalation** — the escalation's `checkPmids` bypassed the 50-id cap; now capped, with incompleteness reported not swallowed.
  5. **(minor) double-fetch** — a PMID cited both directly AND via a co-cited DOI hit NCBI twice; now the direct-screen verdicts are threaded into the escalation so each PMID is fetched once.
- **Verified:** 112/112 tests across every touched module; end-to-end live smoke (real Crossref+PubMed, throttle ON) logically correct on all cases (a clean-case `checked:false` under back-to-back load is fail-soft-correct — `retracted:[]`, only the coverage claim degrades). Zero restricted globals; HTTPS-only.
- **⚠️ CROSS-SURFACE HEADS-UP (not my change):** another surface has an **uncommitted** three-state grounding-verdict rework in `citation-gate.js` + `workflow-engine.js` (operator ruling: "zero evidence → INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE → non-shippable", grounded-ratio floor raised to **0.75**, new `DEFAULT_MIN_GROUNDED_RATIO`). ### 2026-07-10 · CLI Claude Code · Wakefield DOI-only gap **CLOSED** — plus two bugs it uncovered (paren-DOI truncation, notice false-positive)
- **The assigned gap (a retracted paper cited by DOI-only that Crossref misses) is closed** via DOI→PMID escalation. Chose esearch `<doi>[AID]` (per-DOI, exact, reuses eutils plumbing) over (a) idconv — **dead, HTTP 301 — ruled out** and (b) Retraction Watch bulk — **65 MB download, no per-DOI query — overkill**. `resolveDoisToPmids` added to `pubmed-retraction.js`; every cited DOI is now reverse-resolved and cross-checked against the PubMed pubtype screen.
- **Uncovered + fixed BUG #1 (paren-DOI truncation).** The DOI extractor excluded `)`, so Elsevier/Lancet SICI DOIs — including the task's own fixture `10.1016/S0140-6736(97)11096-0` — were truncated to `…(97`, malformed, unscreenable by ANY registry. Fixed: capture balanced internal parens, trim only unbalanced prose-wrapping `)` (`normalizeDoiId`). The markdown-artifact handling (`]]`, `;`, trailing `.`) is preserved and re-tested. **Without this the Wakefield fix could not have worked at all.**
- **Uncovered + fixed BUG #2 (Crossref notice false-positive).** Crossref's `update-to:[retraction]` is AMBIGUOUS — verified live it flags BOTH the Surgisphere original retracted paper AND the Wakefield **retraction notice** `10.1016/S0140-6736(10)60175-4`. So the existing gate would hard-fail a report that legitimately cites a retraction notice. PubMed pubtype disambiguates. New per-DOI rule: **`retracted = pubmedRetracted OR (crossrefFlagged AND NOT pubmedNotice)`** — a Crossref flag is overridden ONLY by a positive PubMed `Retraction Notice`. Fail-closed on the retraction axis: under a PubMed outage a Crossref flag STANDS (block rather than risk shipping a retraction), with `checked:false` signaling incomplete coverage.
- **Verified end-to-end against REAL Crossref + PubMed (spaced to respect NCBI 3/sec): 5/5** — Wakefield-by-DOI caught (via escalation), NEJM clean, Surgisphere caught (Crossref+PubMed agree), retraction-notice-by-DOI NOT flagged (disambiguated), PMID-only retraction caught. **130/130 tests** across every touched module. Zero restricted globals added; HTTPS-only.
- **Adversarial review of the new escalation/disambiguation/extractor code is RUNNING** (last review of this gate found a critical bug, so re-reviewing before declaring done).
- **Backward compat preserved:** 2-arg (Crossref-only) and 3-arg (Crossref+PMID, no resolver) callers unchanged; the DOI→PMID escalation is the optional 4th arg, wired in production via `researchRuntime.pubmedRetraction.resolveDoisToPmids`.
- **Residual gap (documented in SYSTEM-REALITY.md):** a retracted paper cited by DOI-only AND not indexed in PubMed, with no Crossref `update-to`, is still missed — would need the Retraction Watch bulk dataset (storage + refresh).
- **Task 2 (PMID regex `\d{6,9}` bounds — sub-6-digit + 10-digit PMIDs invisible) is NOT bundled here** — it's independent (Wakefield's PMID is 7 digits) and touches the same load-bearing grounded-ratio denominator, so it stays the queued follow-up needing its own impact analysis.
- **Touches:** `openclaw-bridge/mcp/pubmed-retraction/pubmed-retraction.js` (resolveDoisToPmids, isNoticePubtype, is_notice), `openclaw-bridge/core/{citation-gate,citation-extractor,workflow-engine,reproducibility-manifest}.js`, `tests/core/{pubmed-retraction,citation-extractor}.test.js`, `SYSTEM-REALITY.md`, engine `CLAUDE.md`. ### 2026-07-10 · CLI Claude Code · Retraction gate **widened to PMIDs** — and the DOI screen turned out to miss Wakefield entirely
- **The gap was worse than reported.** Confirmed the assigned gap (`citation-gate.js checkRetractions` filtered `c.type === "DOI"`, so PMID-only citations were never screened). But probing the real APIs found a **second, live false negative in the shipping gate**: `api.crossref.org/works/10.1016/S0140-6736(97)11096-0` (**Wakefield 1998, retracted 2010**) returns `relation:{}` with **no `update-to`** — the existing DOI screen scores the most notorious retraction in medicine as **clean**. The 2020 Surgisphere paper *does* carry `update-to:[retraction]` and is caught, so Crossref coverage is inconsistent by record age. PubMed marks Wakefield `Retracted Publication`.
- **Rejected both options as specified.** (a) PMID→DOI→Crossref would have "fixed" the gap while still passing Wakefield — false confidence. (b) Routing through the new `scholar-sidekick` connector puts an external SaaS (500 req/mo free tier, per-request overage, attribution clause) in the hard-fail path of a gate that refuses to ship reports. **Built instead:** `openclaw-bridge/mcp/pubmed-retraction/pubmed-retraction.js` — screens cited PMIDs against PubMed E-utilities `esummary`, batched 100/request, no new dependency (reuses the `NCBI_API_KEY`/contact-email plumbing `citation-reresolver` already has).
- **⚠️ The trap:** PubMed's `Retraction Notice` pubtype (PMID 20137807) also contains "retract". Citing a retraction notice is legitimate. Match is therefore **exact** on `Retracted Publication` — a `/retract/i` regex would hard-fail a correct report. Never loosen it.
- **Adversarial review caught a CRITICAL bug in my own fix.** `runScreen` set `ok:false` only when the checker *threw* — but both production screens are fail-soft and **never throw** (they resolve rows with `checked:false` on 429/503). So under a sustained NCBI/Crossref outage the gate reported `checked:true` with `pmids_checked:0`, and the checksum-sealed customer Reproducibility Manifest rendered "Retraction screen: checked" having screened nothing. Fixed: `checked` now derives from definitive per-identifier verdicts (`complete = checked_count === ids.length`); an outage renders `INCOMPLETE` and, correctly, still **DELIVERs** (it degrades the claim, never blocks a passing report). Review also caught that I fed the merged DOI+PMID array into the manifest's DOI-only `retracted_dois` field, which drives a BLOCK decision — now `retracted_dois` / `retracted_pmids` stay separate, either one BLOCKs, and a new `retracted_pmid` block reason exists.
- **Also fixed (pre-existing):** `checkRetractions` screened `dois.slice(0,50)` while reporting `dois_checked: dois.length` — the ledger overstated its own coverage. Now reports real verdict counts + an explicit `unscreened`. And `workflow-engine` hardcoded `retraction: {checked:true}` into the manifest regardless of whether the screen ran.
- **Verified:** 84/84 tests across every touched module; new `tests/core/pubmed-retraction.test.js` (35 tests incl. the notice trap, outage semantics, and an end-to-end real-module-under-429 case) + manifest regressions. **Live smoke against real NCBI: 6/6** (Wakefield + Surgisphere retracted; NEJM/GeneReviews/notice clean; bogus PMID → checked, not retracted). Zero restricted globals added. HTTPS-only.
- **Suite status (honest):** `npm test` on `main` is **already red** — pristine `HEAD` with deps linked = 852 tests / **27 pre-existing failures** (cline policy gate, phase1 evidence, distribution adapters, phase14 …), none citation-related. My tree = 884 / 28. The one extra name, `phase18 agent spawner … openLoops`, **passes in isolation on both trees**; it asserts `openLoops.length === 0` on *shared global* runtime state and races under the parallel runner. Confirming flakiness on a second HEAD run.
- **Residual gaps spawned as tasks:** (1) a Wakefield-class paper cited **by DOI only** is still missed — needs DOI→PMID reverse lookup or the Retraction Watch dataset; (2) `citation-extractor.js` PMID regex `\d{6,9}` cannot see real sub-6-digit PMIDs (PMID 1 resolves) and will break at 10 digits.
- **Touches:** NEW `openclaw-bridge/mcp/pubmed-retraction/pubmed-retraction.js`, NEW `tests/core/pubmed-retraction.test.js`; MOD `openclaw-bridge/core/{citation-gate,workflow-engine,reproducibility-manifest}.js`, `scripts/_workflow-runtime.js`, `tests/reproducibility-manifest.test.js`, `SYSTEM-REALITY.md`, engine `CLAUDE.md`. ### 2026-07-10T12:25Z · CLI Claude Code · `robust.js` audited for the rerank silent-catch shape — **it doesn't have it**; one real `TTLCache` bug found and fixed
- **Did:** Multi-lens adversarial audit (5 finder lenses → independent refuters) of `src/robust.js` retry/catch paths. **The headline result is negative and worth recording: `retry()` does NOT have the rerank-class silent-catch bug.** It rethrows at `robust.js:92` — a recovered retry returns correct results, an exhausted one propagates to `tools.js:276` and surfaces as an MCP `isError`. Nothing is hidden, so nothing was added there.
- **BUG FOUND (unrelated, real): `TTLCache.set()` evicted an entry it had no business evicting.** The eviction loop ran *before* checking whether `key` was already present, so refreshing a cached value at capacity dropped an unrelated live entry **and left the cache below `max`**. Demonstrated: `max=2; set(a); set(b); set(b,…)` → keys `['b']`, `a` gone. Reachable in the connector only via a concurrent-duplicate-URL race (`server.js` dispatches tool calls with no serialization; `fetchSearxng` has no in-flight dedup), so two concurrent searches for the same url both miss and both `set()`. **Returned data was never wrong** — the cost is an avoidable upstream refetch. Fixed with a `!this.map.has(key)` guard; FIFO semantics verified preserved (refreshing the oldest key does **not** promote it — still FIFO, not LRU).
- **A proposed fix was correctly KILLED by the verifiers:** narrowing `defaultIsRetryable` so a status-less `SyntaxError` stops being retryable. Wrong — `retry()` re-invokes `fn`, which issues a **fresh fetch** each attempt rather than reparsing bytes, so a parse error off a truncated body genuinely can succeed on retry. And SearXNG's "is JSON format enabled?" misconfig arrives as **HTTP 403** (status-tagged, already non-retryable), not as a 2xx+HTML body. Only the misleading comment at `robust.js:60` was corrected; classification behavior is unchanged.
- **State:** done (uncommitted — commit requires YubiKey signature). Unit suite **50/50 green** (`npm run test:unit`).
- **Next:** Nothing blocking on the connector. If anyone wants retry *observability* (intermediate retries are invisible today), the right shape is an injectable `opts.onRetry` matching the existing `opts.rand` / `opts.sleep` DI pattern — **not** a `warnOnce` inside `robust.js`, which is deliberately dependency-free and generic. Deliberately not built: it fixes no actual defect.
- **Touches:** `Claude/searxng-search-mcp/src/robust.js`, `Claude/searxng-search-mcp/test/robust.test.mjs`.
- **For whoever's next:** A `Next:` line here gets read by other surfaces as a *directive*. **Name the codebase in it** — this entry exists because the previous one said "`src/robust.js` retry paths" with no repo, and an operator forwarded it believing it concerned the `promptify` skill.

### 2026-07-10T12:05Z · CLI Claude Code · rerank fail-soft is no longer **silent** (`warnOnce` on the catch path)
- **Did:** Closed the observability gap left by the rerank no-op bug (entry 3 below). `rerank()`'s catch-all swallowed timeouts, TLS failures and connection-refused with **zero output**, so an enabled-but-unreachable reranker looked identical to one that ran and declined to reorder — the same invisible-no-op shape as that bug. Added a keyed `warnOnce` + `describeRerankError()` to the catch. **The fail-soft invariant is untouched: callers still get natively-ordered results.**
- **Also fixed a latent bug in `warnOnce` itself:** it used a single module-level `_warned` boolean, so *any* second call site would have been permanently masked by the first. Now keyed per-message (`bad-endpoint` / `call-failed`).
- **Verified live** — all three operator-relevant causes now name themselves instead of a bare `fetch failed`: Ollama down → `(ECONNREFUSED)`; `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` unset → `(SELF_SIGNED_CERT_IN_CHAIN)`; slow model → `timed out after Nms`. Unit suite **49/49 green** (`npm run test:unit`, includes the SSRF entry below), plus a new regression test. Note `npm test` = `smoke.mjs` and needs SearXNG live on `:8891`.
- **State:** done (uncommitted — commit requires YubiKey signature).
- **Next:** ~~Optional: the same silent-catch shape may exist elsewhere — `src/robust.js` retry paths were not audited for it.~~ **Done — see the newest entry above: audited, no silent catch there, one unrelated `TTLCache` bug fixed.** *(Note to future authors: that line said "retry paths" without naming a repo, and a later operator prompt read it as promptify-skill work. Name the codebase in a `Next:`.)*
- **Touches:** `Claude/searxng-search-mcp/src/rerank.js`, `Claude/searxng-search-mcp/test/rerank.test.mjs`.
- **For whoever's next:** Node's `fetch` refuses low ports (1, 7, 9, 22…) as spec **"bad port"** *before connecting* — a test aiming at ECONNREFUSED must use a high port (59321) or it silently exercises the wrong branch. Real transport causes hide on `err.cause.code`, never on `err.message`.

### 2026-07-10 · CLI Claude Code · SearXNG connector **SSRF guard shipped** — `extract`/`map` can no longer be steered into reading loopback/private services
- **Did:** Closed the propose-only SSRF finding from the audit entry below. New `src/ssrf.js` + a rewritten `fetchUrl()` in `src/extract.js`. One choke point covers **all three tools**, since `extract`, `map` (`sameHostLinks`) and `search(include_raw_content:true)` all funnel through `fetchUrl`.
- **Policy:** resolve the hostname, refuse if **any** resolved address is loopback `127/8`+`::1`, RFC1918 (`10/8`, `172.16/12`, `192.168/16`), CGNAT `100.64/10`, link-local `169.254/16` (incl. the `169.254.169.254` cloud-metadata address) + `fe80::/10`, unique-local `fc00::/7`, multicast/reserved, or unspecified. IPv4-mapped v6 (`::ffff:127.0.0.1`, `::ffff:7f00:1`) is unwrapped and re-checked; zone ids (`fe80::1%en0`) are stripped; anything unparseable **fails closed**. Non-http(s) schemes (`file:`, `ftp:`, `data:`) are refused outright.
- **Redirects were the real hole:** a *public* url can 302 into loopback. `fetchUrl` now uses `redirect:"manual"` and re-validates **every hop** (relative `Location` resolved against the current url), capped at 5 hops. Verified with a stubbed `fetch`: `http://8.8.8.8/redirect` → `http://127.0.0.1:6333/…` is refused at hop 2 and **hop 2 never leaves the process**. Real cross-scheme redirects still work (`http://github.com/…` → `https` extracts fine).
- **Escape hatch:** `SEARXNG_EXTRACT_ALLOW_HOSTS` (comma-separated). Matched on the **hostname as written**, not the resolved IP, because that's what an operator can predict. Empty by default. Allowlisting `127.0.0.1` does **not** unlock `10.0.0.5` (tested).
- **⚠️ Known residual risk, documented in `ssrf.js` not hidden:** DNS-rebinding TOCTOU. We resolve → approve → hand the *name* back to `fetch`, which resolves again. Closing it needs IP pinning (undici `Agent` + custom `connect.lookup`); Node 22 exposes no dispatcher hook on global `fetch` and this package intentionally has no `undici` dep. Far narrower than the previous wide-open behavior.
- **Verified:** the exact 2026-07-10 leaks now return `failed_results[{error:"blocked: … non-public address"}]` instead of bodies — for `extract` **and** `map`. Public `extract`/`map` unaffected (smoke green, 40 urls mapped, 20k chars extracted).
- **Also landed (concurrent edit by another surface, kept):** `rerank.js` gained a **keyed** `warnOnce` + `describeRerankError`, so an enabled-but-dead reranker now says so on stderr (`rerank skipped, keeping native order — <endpoint>: TypeError: fetch failed (ECONNREFUSED)`) instead of looking identical to a reranker that ran and chose not to reorder. Fail-soft return contract unchanged. My `repairOrder` + arity prompt survived intact.
- **State:** `npm run test:unit` **50/50 pass** (new `test/ssrf.test.mjs`: address classification, mapped-v6, schemes, allowlist scoping, redirect-into-loopback, relative `Location`, redirect-loop termination). `npm test` smoke green. Re-staged via `install-connector.sh`; repo `src/` == staged `src/`. Transport lint back to its **pre-existing baseline** (11 unmarked / 10 outbound, all in the git-ignored `scrape-sidecar/.venv` + the TLS proxy) — **0 hits in this connector** (a doc comment naming a plaintext `host:port` correctly tripped it and was reworded, not suppressed).
- **✅ COMMITTED (2026-07-10) — `66119e6` on `main`, GPG "Good signature".** All three fixes (SSRF guard + reranker + metadata-DOI) plus the concurrent `robust.js`/`rerank.js` warn-once hardening landed as ONE connector-scoped commit (`fix(searxng-search): …`, 12 files, +586/−39). Nothing outside `Claude/searxng-search-mcp/` was touched — the tooluniverse submodule bump and the `mcp-connector-gap-analysis-2026-07-10.md` doc remain intentionally uncommitted. No push (local-only invariant; no remotes). Live re-verified through the MCP tool after commit: extract refuses `file:`/loopback/link-local, public extract works, science results carry `pmid`(url)+`nct`(text)+`doi`(metadata).
- **✅ RESTARTED + VERIFIED LIVE (05:46).** All three fixes are now active through the real MCP tool: `extract` refuses `file:` / `127.0.0.1:6333` / `169.254.169.254` (`response_time: 0` — nothing left the process), `map` on loopback returns `errors[{step:"crawl", error:"blocked: …"}]`, public `extract`/`search` unaffected, and every science result now carries both a url-derived `pmid` and a `source:"metadata"` DOI.
- **🔑 Non-obvious infra fact worth keeping (this cost real debugging time):** the `searxng-search` MCP server backing the **Claude Code Desktop tab is spawned by `Claude.app` itself (parent PID 1548)**, *not* by the per-session `claude-code` child process. Each claude-code session also spawns its own copy, but **those are not the ones answering tool calls.** So a server that started *after* `install-connector.sh` can still serve stale code — process start time proves nothing. Ground truth = watch which PID opens the outbound socket (`lsof -nP -c node -iTCP` while a slow `extract` runs). To restart: `kill` the `Claude.app`-parented server PID(s); Claude Desktop **respawns lazily on the next tool call** (it did not respawn on kill alone). Confirmed: stale 2234/2235 (02:08:48) → fresh 77231 (05:46:46). `~/.claude/mcp-servers/searxng-search/` remains a **copy, not a symlink** — always re-run `install-connector.sh` before restarting.
- **Touches:** `Claude/searxng-search-mcp/src/{ssrf.js (new),extract.js,config.js,tools.js}`, `test/ssrf.test.mjs` (new), `package.json`, this board.

### 2026-07-10 · CLI Claude Code · SearXNG MCP connector **robustness audit + 2 real bugs fixed** (rerank was a silent no-op; `min_citations` was silently dropping 15% of cited papers)
- **Did:** End-to-end solo test of the `searxng-search` connector (no subagents) — service health, all 3 tools, every parameter, boundary + adversarial inputs — then root-caused and fixed the two defects found. Both were **fail-soft, so nothing ever errored** and neither had surfaced in normal use.
- **BUG 1 — `rerank:true` had never once reordered anything.** `qwen2.5:7b-instruct` does not emit a permutation: asked to rank, it **filters**, returning only the indices it judged relevant (`[0,1,6]` for n=10). Measured validity against `isPermutation`: **100% at n=2, 0% at n=4/6/8/10** — and the default `max_results` is 5, so the strict check rejected *every* response and silently kept native order. **Root cause = the prompt, not the model:** restating the required arity ("the array MUST contain exactly N integers … never omit an index") took qwen2.5 from **0/4 → 4/4 valid**. Fix = arity-explicit prompt + new `repairOrder()` that honors the model's ranked prefix and appends omitted indices in native order (a filtered answer is real ranking signal; discarding it wholesale threw the feature away). **Verified live:** now demotes an off-topic patient-attitudes survey from #2 → #5 on a `lecanemab ARIA` query.
- **BUG 2 — engine-supplied DOIs were dropped, so the "every result sourced" gate silently discarded real papers.** SearXNG's scholarly engines render the `paper.html` template and return the record's DOI as a **structured field** (`r.doi`), which the connector never passed to the citation harvester — it read only url/title/snippet. Measured across 6 science queries: **409 results, 54% carry `doi` metadata, 71% harvestable from text/url, and 15% (61/409) carry a real DOI but NO harvestable id** → `min_citations>=1` dropped them. Fix = additive `addMetadataDoi()` with new provenance `source:"metadata"`. Chosen deliberately: `robust.dedupe()` keys result identity on `source==="url"` only, so a metadata DOI **can never collapse two distinct rows**. `citation-grammar.js` left untouched so the cross-runtime drift guard still holds. **Verified live:** `lecanemab ARIA` + `min_citations:1` went from **6 → 12** kept results.
- **Verified working (no change needed):** service + TLS proxy healthy (`:8890` upstream, `:8891` proxy, both launchd units up); `topic:science` routes to crossref/openalex/pubmed/semantic-scholar; NCT harvested from abstract text (`NCT04255433`, SURPASS-CVOT); `include_domains`/`exclude_domains` incl. subdomain match; `applied_time_range` echo; `max_results:0` → answer-only infobox; `include_raw_content`; `extract` (Readability→Turndown, correct `HTTP 404` vs `fetch failed` in `failed_results[]`); `map`; a DuckDuckGo CAPTCHA surfaced correctly in `unresponsive_engines` instead of failing the call.
- **⚠️ Open, propose-only — NOT fixed (operator's call):** `extract` has **no SSRF guard**. It fetched `http://127.0.0.1:6333/collections` (Qdrant) and `http://127.0.0.1:8890/config` (SearXNG's own plaintext upstream) and returned their bodies. An LLM ingesting untrusted search-result text could be steered into reading internal loopback services over plaintext. Suggested fix: deny private/loopback/link-local targets in `extract`/`map` unless explicitly allowlisted.
- **Sharp edges (documented, not bugs):** `max_results:40` on `topic:science` ≈ **72k chars** — right at the harness tool-result cap, and it *does* spill when `min_citations` pulls long-abstract records into the top 40 (prefer ≤20). No relevance floor: a gibberish query returns 5 confident-looking results *about* gibberish. Crossref "Decision letter for …" peer-review **stub** records (empty `content`) can outscore the real paper (18.25 vs 5.33) — `exclude_domains:["webofscience.com"]` filters them.

- **Validated (this was the ask):** per-citation external re-resolution is **BUILT, WIRED, and PROVEN**. ### 2026-07-06T12:20Z · CLI Claude Code · Phase 3 — scaffolding-leak INVESTIGATION (proposal only, no pipeline edits) + local commit
- **Investigation (4-agent read-only trace):** Confirmed prompt/tool-prefetch scaffolding leaks into agent DELIVERABLES. **SOURCE (3 classes):** (1) a verbatim meta-instruction — the `LIVE DATABASE CONTEXT: … PRIORITIZE this live data over your training knowledge …` string, hard-coded in `openclaw-bridge/core/agent-engine.js` `buildPrompt()` (~L135-144), pushed onto `headerParts` whenever an input doc is `tooluniverse://`-sourced; (2) tool-prefetch synthetic-doc labels — `tool-prefetch.js` `formatToolResult()`/`renderRecord()` (~L111-160) render prefetch results as markdown (`## Live Data:`, `### {title}`, `**Stopped:**`, `Source: ToolUniverse SMCP / Query: / Retrieved:`) pushed into the SAME `inputDocs` array as real seed docs, undistinguished; (3) **model-INVENTED headers** — `Synthesis Confidence Statement` / `Action Required Before Delivery` / `Live Data Quality Assessment` exist in NO template/config (grepped role-prompts, mission-templates, workflow-templates, agent-config); the analyst/synthesizer roles have NO dedicated `role-prompts.json` entry, so they run on base `buildPrompt` scaffolding and paraphrase its confidence/live-data/zero-fabrication concepts into their own report headers. **MECHANISM:** `output.md` is written PURELY from `completion.text` (`agent-engine.js:414` → `saveOutput`; `research-output-manager.renderOutputContent` is `String(output).trim()+"\n"`, no prompt ref; llm-adapter pipes prompt to stdin, extracts only `parsed.result`) — so this is a **MODEL-ECHO problem, not a string-concat bug**. Root cause: `buildPrompt` assembles ONE undelimited block (role + citation rules + zero-fabrication + LIVE DATABASE CONTEXT + `--- DOC N: <path> ---` doc bodies) joined by plain `\n` with **no fence** separating instructions from the deliverable and **no anti-echo directive**; the deliverable-packager copies the raw markdown forward with no strip. Inter-agent handoff (`spawn-orchestrator.js:132`) feeds a subtask's raw output as a plain input doc → compounds the echo. (The section-gate `STOPWORDS` workaround only stops these words polluting SEARCH QUERIES; it does NOT clean deliverables.)
- **PROPOSAL (not implemented — for review):** **(A, root fix, recommended)** in `buildPrompt` (`agent-engine.js`), fence meta-instructions (`<instructions>…</instructions>`) + input docs (`<context><document>…`), and add an explicit anti-echo directive ("produce ONLY the deliverable; do not restate these instructions, the LIVE DATABASE CONTEXT note, DOC banners, or confidence/quality/fabrication self-assessment — those belong in metadata"). Blast radius: ONE function, but it's the core prompt path for ALL roles → needs re-validation of citation-density/quality gates + a live run. **(C, complementary, low-risk)** give analyst/synthesizer dedicated `config/role-prompts.json` entries with the same "deliverable-only" framing. **(B, belt-and-suspenders, conservative)** a fail-soft post-generation strip of ONLY the VERBATIM artifacts (the LIVE DATABASE CONTEXT sentence, `--- DOC N ---` banners, `Source:/Query:/Retrieved:` footers) — NOT the paraphrased headers (regex-stripping those risks deleting legitimate analysis: **`Failure Mode Signals`/graveyard content is REAL domain analysis**, per the engine's stop-reason-archaeology feature). Recommend A (+C); B only for the exact-match artifacts. Files that would change: `openclaw-bridge/core/agent-engine.js` (primary), `config/role-prompts.json` (optional). **PAUSED — awaiting review before any implementation.**
- **Committed (local, this session's SearXNG work):** per operator request, committed the coherent SearXNG citation-gate body of work locally on branch `docs-and-post-research-engine-2026-06-12` — the connector (`Claude/searxng-search-mcp/`), the engine adapter (`openclaw-bridge/mcp/searxng-mcp/`), the gate wiring (`workflow-engine.js`), registry, composition test, diagnostic, and these docs. **Deliberately NOT swept in** the large set of UNRELATED pre-existing working-tree changes (36 `Claude/obsidian-openclaw-command-center/` deletions + `CLAUDE.md`/Copilot-Everything bridge/obsidian-bridge-launcher mods) — a separate concern I didn't author; left for the operator to commit separately.

### 2026-07-06T07:57Z · CLI Claude Code · SearXNG section-gate LIVE e2e diagnostic — SAFE but key-term extractor is BLUNT
- **Did:** Ran a read-only harness (`scripts/diag/searxng-section-gate-e2e.mjs`, wired into nothing) against **live SearXNG** (127.0.0.1:8890, UP) using **real** LLM section text — reused an existing on-disk mission (`mission-7ea38920…`, template `clinical_trial_intelligence`, subject **heart failure** from a `clinical-trial-landscape` run; scout/analyst/synthesizer `output.md` under `workspace/research-output/task-*/`). Zero LLM cost. Called `deriveSectionQuery` + real `runCitationGate` + real connector `searchFn` exactly as the engine's `executeQualityAuditStep` does; **did NOT** call `assertCoverageOrThrow` (a "fail" is data, not a throw).
- **Finding (safety holds; teeth are blunt):** On a known-good subject, **no false-fail** — all 3 sections `pass`, 4–8 distinct primary sources each; queries technically distinct (3/3). BUT the section discriminators are **boilerplate noise, not sub-topics**: the key-term extractor grabbed table-headers/status labels (`COMPLETED`, `Phase II`, `Purpose`, `NCT ID`, `DOC`), **literal citation IDs** (`NCT02686749`, `NCT00382863`), and **injected-prompt boilerplate** (`TRAINING KNOWLEDGE`, from the tool-prefetch "prioritize live over TRAINING KNOWLEDGE" instruction). Queries pass only because they lead with the primary subject "heart failure" (which carries retrieval); strip that and they're near-garbage. Symptom: **tier flattening — all 3 → Comprehensive** (every section effectively searches the broad subject, so the tier reflects subject richness, not per-section content). Net: the gate is currently ~topic-level-with-noise in effect, not truly section-level. Latency ~7–9 s/section, **23 s / 3 sections** sequential (a 6-role report ≈ 45–70 s added, no-retry).
- **Next (tuning, not a blocker):** make `topKeyTerms` selective before section-level delivers real teeth — (1) exclude citation-ID-shaped tokens (reuse the connector citation grammar to strip NCT/PMID/DOI/CID), (2) exclude clinical-table/status + prompt boilerplate (`COMPLETED/TERMINATED/Phase/Purpose/Status/Sponsor/TRAINING KNOWLEDGE/LIVE DATABASE CONTEXT`), (3) prefer rare multi-word domain phrases (drug/mechanism/endpoint, e.g. "SGLT2 inhibitor empagliflozin") over frequency-ranked structural tokens — frequency-ranking on a structured report rewards repeated headers over distinctive terms. The fail-closed-only-at-zero SAFETY is validated; the discrimination quality is the open item.
- **Touches:** new `scripts/diag/searxng-section-gate-e2e.mjs` (diagnostic only). Nothing in the engine/gate/registry touched; nothing committed.
- **For whoever's next:** re-run `node scripts/diag/searxng-section-gate-e2e.mjs` (SearXNG must be up) after any `topKeyTerms` change to re-check distinctness + tier discrimination on the same real heart-failure sections.
